It’s been getting in the way.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks at work or in your daily routine because of these memory issues?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean, I still get the cleaning done, but it takes me longer than it should. I keep going back to check my logs because I don’t trust that I wrote things down right.\n\nIt’s made me back off from trying for the supervisor track, honestly. And outside of work, I’ve been skipping things I used to do, like neighborhood clean-ups or family get-togethers, because I just don’t feel like I can keep up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down because of these memory issues and changes in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days I do feel down. It’s like this heavy, flat feeling that just follows me around. Even when I’m just pushing my cart down an empty hallway at work, or sitting with my family trying to act normal, it’s still there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. I feel tense sometimes, sure, but I haven’t really noticed being on edge all the time. It’s more just… hard to think clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It’s like my head won’t stay with what I’m doing. I’ll be doing inventory and then I lose track, or I’ll go to the supply closet and forget what I went in there for. Even when I’m studying for the supervisor certification, I end up reading the same line over and over because it just doesn’t stick.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think people usually tell me I’m not making sense. Sometimes I have to pause to find the right words, but I haven’t noticed it being a big problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disjointed or don't follow a logical order?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. My thoughts feel slow or kind of foggy sometimes, but I wouldn’t say they’re out of order or not logical. I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble finding the right words when speaking?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah, but I don’t think it’s a major thing. I might pause or stumble a bit trying to get the word I want, but I usually get there. It’s not something I’d say happens all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. They can feel slow sometimes, or I might lose my train of thought for a second, but I don’t usually feel like my thoughts are disconnected from each other.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining focus on tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s actually a big part of why I came in. My head just doesn’t stay on task like it used to. I’ll be doing inventory at work and lose track, or I’ll go to the supply closet and forget what I went in there for. And when I try to study for the supervisor certification, I keep reading the same line over and over without it sticking.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that don't make sense to you or seem to be fragmented?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, my thoughts can feel kind of slow or hard to keep on sometimes, but I wouldn’t say they don’t make sense to me or feel fragmented.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty organizing your thoughts when trying to convey them to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. It’s not like my thoughts don’t make sense, but when I’m trying to explain something, I can lose the thread or have trouble keeping it in order. That’s part of why I came in—my head just doesn’t stay on task anymore. Like with inventory, or studying for the supervisor certification, I’ll keep rereading the same line or forget why I went to the supply closet.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I might shift around if I’m uncomfortable, but I wouldn’t say I feel especially restless or fidgety most of the time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

They can feel slow sometimes, or I might lose my train of thought for a second, but I don’t usually feel like my thoughts are disconnected from each other.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining focus on tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s actually a big part of why I came in. My head just doesn’t stay on task like it used to. I’ll be doing inventory at work and lose track, or I’ll go to the supply closet and forget what I went in there for. And when I try to study for the supervisor certification, I keep reading the same line over and over without it sticking.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that don't make sense to you or seem to be fragmented?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, my thoughts can feel kind of slow or hard to keep on sometimes, but I wouldn’t say they don’t make sense to me or feel fragmented.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty organizing your thoughts when trying to convey them to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. It’s not like my thoughts don’t make sense, but when I’m trying to explain something, I can lose the thread or have trouble keeping it in order. That’s part of why I came in—my head just doesn’t stay on task anymore. Like with inventory, or studying for the supervisor certification, I’ll keep rereading the same line or forget why I went to the supply closet.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I might shift around if I’m uncomfortable, but I wouldn’t say I feel especially restless or fidgety most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble putting your thoughts together in a coherent way when speaking or writing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do sometimes. Not like I can’t make sense at all, but it’s harder to pull my thoughts together and keep them going in order. That’s actually why I came in—my head just doesn’t stay on task anymore. I’ll be doing inventory or trying to study for the supervisor certification, and I keep reading the same line over and over, or I’ll go to the supply closet and forget what I went there for.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s not something I’d say happens all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. They can feel slow sometimes, or I might lose my train of thought for a second, but I don’t usually feel like my thoughts are disconnected from each other.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining focus on tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s actually a big part of why I came in. My head just doesn’t stay on task like it used to. I’ll be doing inventory at work and lose track, or I’ll go to the supply closet and forget what I went in there for. And when I try to study for the supervisor certification, I keep reading the same line over and over without it sticking.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that don't make sense to you or seem to be fragmented?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, my thoughts can feel kind of slow or hard to keep on sometimes, but I wouldn’t say they don’t make sense to me or feel fragmented.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty organizing your thoughts when trying to convey them to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. It’s not like my thoughts don’t make sense, but when I’m trying to explain something, I can lose the thread or have trouble keeping it in order. That’s part of why I came in—my head just doesn’t stay on task anymore. Like with inventory, or studying for the supervisor certification, I’ll keep rereading the same line or forget why I went to the supply closet.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don’t think I’m meaning to avoid people, but it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often struggle to pay attention to details?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. That’s a big part of why I came in.
